1	use std::path::PathBuf;
2	use std::process::Stdio;
3	
4	use clap::Args;
5	use log::{debug, info};
6	
7	use crate::common::run;
8	
9	/// Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo.
10	///
11	/// Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:
12	/// --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,
13	/// --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.
14	/// Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.
15	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
16	pub struct FinalizeArgs {
17	    /// Path to jj repo
18	    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
19	    pub repo: PathBuf,
20	
21	    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
22	    #[arg(long, value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET", default_missing_value = "@,@-", num_args = 0..=1)]
23	    pub squash: Option<String>,
24	
25	    /// Seconds to wait before squashing
26	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0)]
27	    pub delay: f64,
28	
29	    /// Existing bookmark to push to remote
30	    #[arg(long, value_name = "BOOKMARK")]
31	    pub push: Option<String>,
32	
33	    /// Detach and run in the background
34	    #[arg(long)]
35	    pub detach: bool,
36	
37	    /// Internal: run exec path (used by detach)
38	    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
39	    pub exec: bool,
40	}
41	
42	/// Parsed squash spec: source and target revisions.
43	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
44	pub struct SquashSpec {
45	    pub source: String,
46	    pub target: String,
47	}
48	
49	impl SquashSpec {
50	    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
51	        let parts: Vec<&str> = s.split(',').collect();
52	        if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {
53	            return Err(format!(
54	                "invalid --squash value '{s}': expected SOURCE,TARGET (e.g. @,@-)"
55	            ));
56	        }
57	        Ok(SquashSpec {
58	            source: parts[0].to_string(),
59	            target: parts[1].to_string(),
60	        })
61	    }
62	}
63	
64	#[derive(Debug)]
65	pub struct FinalizeOpts {
66	    pub repo: PathBuf,
67	    pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,
68	    pub delay_secs: f64,
69	    pub bookmark: Option<String>,
70	    pub push: bool,
71	    pub log: Option<PathBuf>,
72	    pub detach: bool,
73	    pub exec: bool,
74	}
75	
76	impl FinalizeArgs {
77	    pub fn into_opts(self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> Result<FinalizeOpts, String> {
78	        let squash = self.squash.map(|s| SquashSpec::parse(&s)).transpose()?;
79	        let push = self.push.is_some();
80	        let bookmark = self.push;
81	        // Resolve to absolute path so detached child works regardless of cwd
82	        let repo = std::fs::canonicalize(&self.repo)
83	            .map_err(|e| format!("cannot resolve repo path '{}': {e}", self.repo.display()))?;
84	        Ok(FinalizeOpts {
85	            repo,
86	            squash,
87	            delay_secs: self.delay,
88	            bookmark,
89	            push,
90	            log,
91	            detach: self.detach,
92	            exec: self.exec,
93	        })
94	    }
95	}
96	
97	fn finalize_exec(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
98	    debug!("finalize_exec: starting opts={opts:?}");
99	    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();
100	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
101	
102	    // Squash if requested
103	    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {
104	        let delay = std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(opts.delay_secs);
105	        debug!("finalize_exec: sleeping {delay:?}");
106	        std::thread::sleep(delay);
107	
108	        run(
109	            "jj",
110	            &[
111	                "squash",
112	                "--ignore-immutable",
113	                "--use-destination-message",
114	                "--from",
115	                &sq.source,
116	                "--into",
117	                &sq.target,
118	                "-R",
119	                &repo_str,
120	            ],
121	            cwd,
122	        )?;
123	    }
124	
125	    // Set bookmark and push if requested
126	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = opts.bookmark {
127	        // Verify bookmark exists — don't silently create new ones
128	        let result = run("jj", &["bookmark", "list", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str], cwd)?;
129	        if result.is_empty() {
130	            return Err(format!("bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist").into());
131	        }
132	
133	        let rev = opts
134	            .squash
135	            .as_ref()
136	            .map(|sq| sq.target.as_str())
137	            .unwrap_or("@"); // OK: no squash spec → bookmark points at current @
138	        run(
139	            "jj",
140	            &["bookmark", "set", bookmark, "-r", rev, "-R", &repo_str],
141	            cwd,
142	        )?;
143	
144	        if opts.push {
145	            run(
146	                "jj",
147	                &["git", "push", "--bookmark", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
148	                &opts.repo,
149	            )?;
150	        }
151	    }
152	
153	    debug!("finalize_exec: done");
154	    Ok(())
155	}
156	
157	pub fn build_exec_args(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Vec<String> {
158	    let mut args = vec![
159	        "finalize".to_string(),
160	        "--exec".to_string(),
161	        "--repo".to_string(),
162	        opts.repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
163	    ];
164	    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {
165	        args.push("--squash".to_string());
166	        args.push(format!("{},{}", sq.source, sq.target));
167	        args.push("--delay".to_string());
168	        args.push(opts.delay_secs.to_string());
169	    }
170	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = opts.bookmark {
171	        args.push("--push".to_string());
172	        args.push(bookmark.clone());
173	    }
174	    if let Some(ref log) = opts.log {
175	        args.push("--log".to_string());
176	        args.push(log.to_string_lossy().to_string());
177	    }
178	    args
179	}
180	
181	/// Spawn a detached child process with `--exec` and return immediately.
182	/// The child re-enters `main()`, parses `--exec`, and `finalize()` routes
183	/// it to `finalize_exec()` where the actual work happens.
184	fn detach(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
185	    debug!("detach: parent starting opts={opts:?}");
186	
187	    let exe = std::env::current_exe()?;
188	    let args = build_exec_args(opts);
189	    debug!("detach: exe={exe:?} args={args:?}");
190	
191	    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(exe);
192	    cmd.args(&args).stdin(Stdio::null());
193	
194	    #[cfg(unix)]
195	    {
196	        use std::os::unix::process::CommandExt;
197	        unsafe {
198	            cmd.pre_exec(|| {
199	                libc::setsid();
200	                Ok(())
201	            });
202	        }
203	    }
204	
205	    let child = cmd.spawn()?;
206	    debug!("detach: spawned child pid={}", child.id());
207	    match &opts.log {
208	        Some(log) => info!(
209	            "finalize: detached (pid {}), log: {}",
210	            child.id(),
211	            log.display()
212	        ),
213	        None => info!("finalize: detached (pid {})", child.id()),
214	    }
215	    Ok(())
216	}
217	
218	pub fn finalize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
219	    // Nothing to do — show help hint
220	    if opts.squash.is_none() && !opts.push {
221	        return Err("nothing to do (see --help for options)".into());
222	    }
223	
224	    debug!("finalize: entry opts={opts:?}");
225	    let result = if opts.detach && !opts.exec {
226	        detach(opts)
227	    } else {
228	        finalize_exec(opts)
229	    };
230	    match &result {
231	        Ok(()) => debug!("finalize: exit ok"),
232	        Err(e) => debug!("finalize: exit err={e}"),
233	    }
234	    result
235	}
236

> AGENT

I see the issue. Looking at finalize.rs and the log, the failure was in the detached child (push failed because `main@origin` is non-tracking), but:

1. **Parent exits successfully** the moment it spawns the child, so the bot sees exit code 0.
2. **Child output only goes to the log file** — never stdout of the active session.
3. By the time the bot could check, the session is already finalized.

This is a fundamental tension with `--detach`: the thing that makes it useful (survives session-end) is also what hides failures. "Always send to stdout" can't fix the detached case because the parent is already gone.

Three orthogonal fixes, roughly in order of leverage:

**A) Pre-flight validation in the parent, before detach.** The specific failure here (non-tracking remote bookmark) is knowable *before* we detach — we could run `jj bookmark list --tracked` or similar and fail loudly with a non-zero exit code synchronously. Same for: bookmark exists, squash source/target resolve, `.jj` repo exists. This catches probably 80% of real failures with a visible exit code.

**B) Status marker file.** Child writes `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize.status` (or inside the repo) with exit code + last error. Next bot session checks it on startup and surfaces any prior failure. Cheap, closes the visibility gap for post-detach failures (network errors, races).

**C) Optionally tee child output to parent's stderr** when not detached, and always also log when a path is given. Low-value for the detached path but good hygiene.

My recommendation: **A + B**. A catches the common errors loudly and synchronously; B is the safety net for anything the parent can't know (push/network). Would also bump the finalize log level so the non-tracking case is unambiguous.

Want me to turn this into a plan with a version bump?

61	/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
62	///
63	/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
64	/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
65	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
66	pub struct DotSpec {
67	    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
68	    pub rev: String,
69	    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
70	    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
71	    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
72	    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
73	}
74	
75	/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
76	///
77	/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
78	/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
79	/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
80	/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
81	pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
82	    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
83	        DotSpec {
84	            rev: inner.to_string(),
85	            desc_count: None,
86	            anc_count: None,
87	        }
88	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
89	        DotSpec {
90	            rev: inner.to_string(),
91	            desc_count: None,
92	            anc_count: Some(0),
93	        }
94	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
95	        DotSpec {
96	            rev: inner.to_string(),
97	            desc_count: Some(0),
98	            anc_count: None,
99	        }
100	    } else {
101	        DotSpec {
102	            rev: rev.to_string(),
103	            desc_count: Some(0),
104	            anc_count: Some(0),
105	        }
106	    }
107	}
108	
109	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
110	///
111	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
112	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
113	pub fn resolve_spec(
114	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
115	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
116	    flag_rev: &str,
117	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
118	    default_rev: &str,
119	) -> DotSpec {
120	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
121	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
122	        flag_rev
123	    } else {
124	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev) // OK: positional absent → fall back to CLI default
125	    };
126	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
127	
128	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
129	        flag_limit
130	    } else {
131	        pos_count
132	    };
133	
134	    if let Some(c) = count {
135	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
136	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
137	        if both_open {
138	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
139	            let desc_n = n / 2;
140	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
141	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
142	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
143	        } else {
144	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
145	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
146	            }
147	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
148	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
149	            }
150	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
151	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
152	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
153	            }
154	        }
155	    }
156	
157	    spec
158	}

160	/// Run a shell command. Logs the command and output at debug level.
161	/// Returns stdout on success.
162	pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
163	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
164	    debug!("$ {cmd} {args_str}");
165	    trace!("cwd: {}", cwd.display());
166	    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)
167	        .args(args)
168	        .current_dir(cwd)
169	        .output()
170	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run {cmd}: {e}"))?;
171	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
172	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
173	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
174	        debug!("  {stdout}");
175	    }
176	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
177	        debug!("  {stderr}");
178	    }
179	    if !output.status.success() {
180	        return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
181	    }
182	    Ok(stdout)
183	}

199	/// Display a prompt, read a line from stdin, and log both together.
200	/// Returns the trimmed response.
201	pub fn prompt(msg: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
202	    use std::io::Write;
203	    eprint!("{msg}");
204	    std::io::stderr().flush()?;
205	    let mut response = String::new();
206	    std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;
207	    let trimmed = response.trim().to_string();
208	    info!("{msg}{trimmed}");
209	    Ok(trimmed)
210	}

218	pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
219	    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
220	        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
221	    } else {

226	/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
227	///
228	/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
229	pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
230	    if n == 0 {
231	        return s.to_string();
232	    }
233	    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
234	    let mut lines = s.lines();
235	    let mut result = match lines.next() {
236	        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
237	        None => return String::new(),
238	    };
239	    for line in lines {
240	        result.push('\n');
241	        if !line.is_empty() {
242	            result.push_str(&pad);
243	        }
244	        result.push_str(line);
245	    }
246	    result
247	}

249	/// Extract the ochid trailer value from a commit description, if present.
250	pub fn extract_ochid(commit: &Commit) -> Option<String> {
251	    for line in commit.description().lines().rev() {
252	        let trimmed = line.trim();
253	        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
254	            return Some(value.trim().to_string());
255	        }
256	    }
257	    None
258	}

260	/// Format: changeID ochid(padded) first-line (single line).
261	///
262	/// ochid column is left-padded to `width` characters. If no ochid, a blank
263	/// placeholder of that width is used.
264	pub fn format_commit_with_ochid(commit: &Commit, width: usize) -> String {
265	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
266	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
267	    let ochid = extract_ochid(commit).unwrap_or_default(); // OK: no ochid trailer → empty string
268	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
269	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
270	        "(no description set)"
271	    } else {
272	        first_line
273	    };
274	    format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {title}")
275	}
276	
277	/// Format: just the short changeID.
278	pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
279	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
280	    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
281	}
282	
283	/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
284	pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
285	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
286	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
287	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
288	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
289	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
290	    if first_line.is_empty() {
291	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
292	    } else {
293	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
294	    }
295	}
296	
297	/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
298	pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
299	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
300	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
301	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
302	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
303	    let desc = commit.description();
304	    if desc.is_empty() {
305	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
306	    } else {
307	        let mut lines = desc.lines();
308	        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
309	        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
310	        for line in lines {
311	            result.push('\n');
312	            result.push_str(line);
313	        }
314	        result
315	    }
316	}

318	/// Collect commit IDs for `..x..` (both directions) or any dot notation.
319	///
320	/// Returns `(commit_ids, anchor_index)` — the IDs in display order
321	/// (descendants closest to anchor, anchor, ancestors closest to anchor)
322	/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.
323	///
324	/// - `desc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest descendants, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
325	/// - `anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
326	pub fn collect_ids(
327	    workspace: &Workspace,
328	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
329	    rev: &str,
330	    desc_count: Option<usize>,
331	    anc_count: Option<usize>,
332	) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
333	    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;
334	    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
335	        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{rev}'").into());
336	    }
337	    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
338	    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
339	
340	    // Descendants (closest to anchor)
341	    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
342	    if desc_count != Some(0) {
343	        let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{rev}::"))?;
344	        desc_ids = descendant_ids
345	            .into_iter()
346	            .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)
347	            .collect();
348	        if let Some(n) = desc_count {
349	            let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(n);
350	            desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
351	        }
352	    }
353	
354	    // Ancestors (closest to anchor)
355	    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
356	    if anc_count != Some(0) {
357	        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{rev}"))?;
358	        let limit = anc_count.unwrap_or(usize::MAX); // OK: no --ancestors limit → unbounded
359	        let mut count = 0;
360	        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {
361	            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {
362	                continue;
363	            }
364	            anc_ids.push(commit_id);
365	            count += 1;
366	            if count >= limit {
367	                break;
368	            }
369	        }
370	    }
371	
372	    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();
373	    let mut result = desc_ids;
374	    result.push(anchor_id.clone());
375	    result.extend(anc_ids);
376	    Ok((result, anchor_index))
377	}

379	/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.
380	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
381	pub enum Header {
382	    /// Show `deco path deco` header per repo.
383	    Label(String),
384	    /// No header at all.
385	    None,
386	}
387	
388	/// Resolve `-l` / `-L` flags into a `Header`.
389	///
390	/// `-L` (no header) takes precedence over `-l` (label decoration).
391	pub fn resolve_header(label: &str, suppress: bool) -> Header {
392	    if suppress {
393	        Header::None
394	    } else {
395	        Header::Label(label.to_string())
396	    }
397	}
398	
399	/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `["."]`, and run a closure for each.
400	///
401	/// Header style controls output between repos.
402	/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.
403	pub fn for_each_repo<F>(
404	    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],
405	    header: &Header,
406	    mut body: F,
407	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
408	where
409	    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
410	{
411	    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {
412	        vec![PathBuf::from(".")]
413	    } else {
414	        raw_repos
415	            .iter()
416	            .flat_map(|p| {
417	                p.to_string_lossy()
418	                    .split(',')
419	                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
420	                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
421	            })
422	            .collect()
423	    };
424	    let multi = repos.len() > 1;
425	    let mut first = true;
426	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
427	
428	    for repo_path in &repos {
429	        if multi {
430	            match header {
431	                Header::Label(deco) => {
432	                    if !first {
433	                        info!("");
434	                    }
435	                    info!(
436	                        "{}",
437	                        bold(&format!("{deco} {} {deco}", repo_path.display()))
438	                    );
439	                }
440	                Header::None => {}
441	            }
442	        }
443	        first = false;
444	
445	        match load_repo(repo_path) {
446	            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {
447	                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {
448	                    error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
449	                    errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
450	                }
451	            }
452	            Err(e) => {
453	                error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
454	                errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
455	            }
456	        }
457	    }
458	
459	    if errors.is_empty() {
460	        Ok(())
461	    } else {
462	        Err(format!("{} repo(s) had errors", errors.len()).into())
463	    }
464	}
465	
466	pub fn load_repo(
467	    path: &Path,
468	) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
469	    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
470	    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
471	    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
472	    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
473	
474	    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
475	    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
476	
477	    Ok((workspace, repo))
478	}
479	
480	/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
481	pub fn resolve_revset(
482	    workspace: &Workspace,
483	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
484	    revset_str: &str,
485	) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
486	    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
487	    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
488	    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
489	    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
490	    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
491	        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
492	        base: workspace_root,
493	    };
494	    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
495	    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
496	        path_converter: &path_converter,
497	        workspace_name,
498	    };
499	    let context = RevsetParseContext {
500	        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
501	        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
502	        user_email: "",
503	        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
504	        default_ignored_remote: None,
505	        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
506	        use_glob_by_default: false,
507	        extensions: &extensions,
508	        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
509	    };
510	
511	    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
512	    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
513	
514	    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
515	    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
516	    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
517	    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
518	
519	    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
520	    for result in revset.iter() {
521	        commit_ids.push(result?);
522	    }
523	    Ok(commit_ids)
524	}

533	        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "@");
534	        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, Some(0));
535	        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, Some(0));

1	use std::io::Write;
2	use std::sync::Mutex;
3	
4	use log::{Level, LevelFilter, Log, Metadata, Record};
5	
6	/// Simple logger that routes by level:
7	/// - info and above → stdout (user-facing progress)
8	/// - debug/trace → stderr (only when verbose at that level)
9	/// - warn/error → stderr (always)
10	/// - log file captures all enabled levels
11	pub struct CliLogger {
12	    /// The verbose level for stderr output (None = no verbose)
13	    stderr_level: Option<LevelFilter>,
14	    log_file: Option<Mutex<std::fs::File>>,
15	}
16	
17	impl CliLogger {
18	    pub fn new(stderr_level: Option<LevelFilter>, log_path: Option<&str>) -> Self {
19	        let log_file = log_path.map(|path| {
20	            Mutex::new(
21	                std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
22	                    .create(true)
23	                    .append(true)
24	                    .open(path)
25	                    .expect("failed to open log file"),
26	            )
27	        });
28	        CliLogger {
29	            stderr_level,
30	            log_file,
31	        }
32	    }
33	
34	    /// Initialize as the global logger.
35	    /// `verbose`: 0 = off, 1 = debug, 2+ = trace
36	    pub fn init(verbose: u8, log_path: Option<&str>) {
37	        let stderr_level = match verbose {
38	            0 => None,
39	            1 => Some(LevelFilter::Debug),
40	            _ => Some(LevelFilter::Trace),
41	        };
42	        let has_log_file = log_path.is_some();
43	        let logger = Box::new(CliLogger::new(stderr_level, log_path));
44	
45	        // Max level: stderr_level if set, otherwise info
46	        // Log file captures whatever is enabled, not more
47	        let _ = has_log_file;
48	        let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
49	        log::set_max_level(max);
50	        log::set_boxed_logger(logger).expect("failed to set logger");
51	    }
52	}
53	
54	impl Log for CliLogger {
55	    fn enabled(&self, metadata: &Metadata) -> bool {
56	        // Always allow info and above
57	        if metadata.level() <= Level::Info {
58	            return true;
59	        }
60	        // Allow if stderr verbose is at this level
61	        if let Some(level) = self.stderr_level
62	            && metadata.level() <= level
63	        {
64	            return true;
65	        }
66	        false
67	    }
68	
69	    fn log(&self, record: &Record) {
70	        if !self.enabled(record.metadata()) {
71	            return;
72	        }
73	
74	        let msg = format!("{}", record.args());
75	
76	        // Write to log file if configured — captures all levels
77	        if let Some(ref file) = self.log_file
78	            && let Ok(mut f) = file.lock()
79	        {
80	            let _ = writeln!(f, "[{:<5}] {}: {}", record.level(), record.target(), msg);
81	        }
82	
83	        match record.level() {
84	            // User-facing progress → stdout
85	            Level::Info => println!("{msg}"),
86	            // Errors and warnings → stderr always
87	            Level::Error => eprintln!("error: {msg}"),
88	            Level::Warn => eprintln!("warn: {msg}"),
89	            // Debug/trace → stderr only if verbose allows this level
90	            Level::Debug | Level::Trace => {
91	                if let Some(level) = self.stderr_level
92	                    && record.level() <= level
93	                {
94	                    for line in msg.lines() {
95	                        eprintln!("  {line}");
96	                    }
97	                }
98	            }
99	        }
100	    }
101	
102	    fn flush(&self) {
103	        use std::io;
104	        let _ = io::stdout().flush();
105	        let _ = io::stderr().flush();
106	        if let Some(ref file) = self.log_file
107	            && let Ok(mut f) = file.lock()
108	        {
109	            let _ = f.flush();
110	        }
111	    }
112	}
113

> AGENT

Good question — let me trace the actual wiring before answering.

The child *already* inherits stdout/stderr (only stdin is nulled in `finalize.rs:192`). The logger in `logging.rs:85-88` writes INFO to stdout and ERROR to stderr in addition to the log file. So the fd inheritance is there — but two things defeat it in practice:

**1. The parent has already exited.** With `--detach`, the parent spawns the child, logs "finalize: detached (pid …)", and returns 0. The Bash tool (or whatever invoked vc-x1) sees exit 0 and moves on. The child's later stderr write goes… somewhere (terminal buffer, or a closed pipe if the caller captured output, causing SIGPIPE).

**2. The bot can't observe post-return output.** In the Claude Code case specifically, the Bash tool returns as soon as the parent exits. Anything the child prints afterward doesn't come back as tool output, so the bot can't react. Worse, if Bash closed its read pipe on parent exit, the child's writes to stderr may be silently dropped. That's probably why you only saw the log file.

**So "inherit fds" is necessary but not sufficient.** The real issue is the asymmetry: the parent's exit code and output are what the caller reacts to, but the work happens after the parent is gone.

Also worth noting: the detach+delay is load-bearing. The 10s window is *specifically* to capture trailing writes the bot session itself causes on shutdown. If we go synchronous, we lose that. So we can't just drop `--detach`.

That brings us back to:

- **Pre-flight in the parent**: check bookmark tracking / existence / squash resolvability *before* detaching, so that class of errors surfaces synchronously with a non-zero exit and real stderr. The specific failure you hit (`Non-tracking remote bookmark main@origin`) is a perfect candidate — 100% knowable upfront.
- **Status marker for post-detach failures**: write a result file when the child finishes; next vc-x1 invocation (or an explicit `finalize-status` check) reads it and complains loudly.

fd inheritance is a small correctness nit — we should probably also redirect the child's stdout/stderr to the log file explicitly (rather than relying on inherited-but-orphaned pipes) so output isn't lost to SIGPIPE. But it doesn't by itself solve the visibility problem.

> AGENT

Got it. Here's a concrete plan. Version bump first — I'd suggest **0.33.0** since this is a behavioral change to a critical command. Happy to do single-step or multi-step (e.g. dev1 pre-flight, dev2 logging, dev3 status marker, then 0.33.0) — your call.

**1. Pre-flight validation in the parent (before detach)**

In `finalize.rs` before `detach()`:
- If `--push`, run `jj bookmark list --tracked <bookmark> -R <repo>` (or inspect via jj-lib) and confirm the bookmark both exists AND tracks the intended remote. Catch the exact error you hit.
- If `--squash`, resolve source/target revsets via `resolve_revset` to catch bad input.
- Any failure → return `Err` synchronously; parent exits non-zero with visible stderr. No child spawned.

**2. Logger & subprocess output always to terminal + log file**

- `common::run()` currently demotes subprocess stdout/stderr to `debug!`, so without `-v` the user never sees `jj`'s error text. Change: on non-zero exit, log captured stderr at `error!` (so it surfaces). On success with output, log at `info!` or keep `debug!` — your call, but failures must surface.
- Confirm the `CliLogger` guarantee: every enabled record always goes to both stream AND log file. (It already does — this is more audit than change.)
- Consider: for the detached child, explicitly redirect its stdout/stderr in `detach()` to the log file via `Stdio::from(File)` rather than leaving them inherited-and-orphaned. The *terminal* output from the detached child will be lost regardless when invoked via a captured pipe (Bash tool) — but the log file becomes authoritative and reliable.

**3. Post-detach result visibility (new)**

The parent returns before the child is done, so exit code alone can't reflect child result. Options:

- **Status file**: child writes `~/.cache/vc-x1/finalize.status` (or alongside the log) with exit code + last error on completion. On every subsequent `vc-x1` invocation, check for a recent unread status and print a prominent warning to stderr if failed. Mark as read once displayed.
- Alternative: foreground-wait mode where the bot polls `vc-x1 finalize-status` as its final tool call. Simpler, explicit, bot-controlled. But requires changing the session-end workflow in CLAUDE.md.

I'd go status file + auto-check on next invocation. Cheap, invisible when healthy, loud when broken — and it works for both the bot and interactive use.

**Version bump preference?** Single-step 0.33.0, or multi-step dev1/dev2/dev3 rolling up to 0.33.0?

> AGENT

Agreed on 1 & 2. On the "new terminal" angle — actually the child *can* write to a terminal, just not through inherited pipes. Cleaner options:

**Option A: Reconnect via `/dev/tty`**

The parent opens `/dev/tty` (the user's current terminal) before detaching and passes that fd to the child as stdout/stderr. The terminal stays alive independently of the parent process — so even after the parent returns, the child's writes land in the same terminal the user is looking at. No new window, no GUI dependency.

```rust
let tty = std::fs::OpenOptions::new().read(true).write(true).open("/dev/tty").ok();
match tty {
    Some(f) => { cmd.stdout(Stdio::from(f.try_clone()?)).stderr(Stdio::from(f)); }
    None    => { cmd.stdout(Stdio::null()).stderr(Stdio::null()); } // piped (bot) case
}
```

When `/dev/tty` can't open (Claude Code's Bash tool, cron, CI), fallback to null + log file.

**Option B: Spawn a new terminal window** (`xterm -e …`, `gnome-terminal --`, `kitty …`)

Works but brings headaches: need DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY, need to pick a terminal emulator (user's?), doesn't work over SSH without X forwarding, no Mac equivalent, user has to switch windows.

**My recommendation: A.** It's the simplest path to "I want to see the information in my existing terminal" — and it degrades gracefully. Combine with the **status marker file** (for the pipe-invoked bot case where there's no tty at all) and you have full coverage:

- Interactive terminal → `/dev/tty` → user sees output live
- Bot / cron / pipe → log file + status marker → next `vc-x1` invocation surfaces failures loudly

**Revised plan summary:**
1. Pre-flight validation in parent (bookmark exists+tracks, squash revsets resolve) — synchronous non-zero exit with visible stderr
2. `common::run()` — subprocess output at `info!`, failures at `error!`
3. Detached child: open `/dev/tty` in parent, pass to child as stdout/stderr; fallback to null+log-file
4. Status marker file written by child on completion; checked & surfaced by any subsequent `vc-x1` invocation

**Version: single-step 0.33.0 or multi-step (dev1 pre-flight → dev2 logging → dev3 tty → dev4 status, roll to 0.33.0)?**

15 missed some clone/show duplicates. Final count confirmed during edits.)

### `symlink.rs:44` decision

`std::fs::read_link(path).unwrap_or_default()` — reachable only if
`path.symlink_metadata()` just said `is_symlink() == true`. A TOCTOU
race (symlink removed between metadata and read_link calls) could
fire it; falling back to empty `PathBuf` means the caller's subsequent
comparison against the expected target will fail and the symlink gets
recreated. That is acceptable behavior. Keep the default, document
with `// OK: …`.

### Test code

~54 `.unwrap()` calls in `#[cfg(test)]` modules left as-is. Tests
panicking on setup failure is the correct behavior and idiomatic Rust.

### Version

Single-step bump to `0.32.0`. Mechanical doc-only change, no behavior
difference, one commit.

# Chores-04

## Audit `unwrap`/`unwrap_or` usage (0.32.0)

Survey every `unwrap*` site in `src/` (non-test). Classify each, then
annotate with a trailing `// OK: …` comment that justifies why the
call is acceptable. This is a documentation pass — no behavioral
changes — and a convention we can extend to future code.

### Comment convention

- `// OK: <specific reason>` — when there's a real precondition,
  invariant, or domain reason worth capturing
- `// OK: obvious` — when the default is self-evident from context
  (e.g. `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title)

Bare `// OK` is avoided because it reads like a truncated comment.
Abbreviations like `SE` are avoided because they require a decoder
ring for anyone reading the code out of context.

Tests are left alone. `#[cfg(test)]` `.unwrap()` panics on failure,
which is the correct test behavior.

### Documentation home

Dev-facing conventions live in `notes/README.md` (alongside existing
"Versioning during development" and "Todo format" sections). User-facing
`/README.md` gets a small `## Contributing` section pointing at
`notes/`. `CLAUDE.md` adds a one-line reference so the bot sees the
same convention.

- `notes/README.md` — new `## Code Conventions` section with the
  `// OK: …` rule and examples
- `/README.md` — new `## Contributing` section with link to `notes/`
- `CLAUDE.md` — one-line reference to `notes/README.md#code-conventions`

### Library `.unwrap()` (one site)

`src/desc_helpers.rs:157` — inside a `match matches.len()` with arm
`1 =>`, so `matches.len() == 1` is proven. Refactor to block form,
add `#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so we can enable the project-wide
lint later without this site firing, and an `// OK: …` comment.

```rust
1 => {
    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
    // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
    Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
}
```

25	
26	## Versioning during development
27	
28	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
29	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
30	we go.
31	
32	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
33	
34	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
35	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
36	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
37	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
38	
39	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
40	
41	For multi-step:
42	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
43	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
44	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
45	
46	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
47	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
48	commits were exploratory vs final.
49	
50	## Code Conventions
51	
52	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls
53	
54	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`, `.unwrap_or_default()`,
55	or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing `// OK: …` comment that justifies
56	why the call is acceptable.
57	
58	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition, invariant, or
59	  domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't self-evident.
60	- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
61	  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).
62	
63	Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment). Abbreviations
64	(e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder ring for readers
65	seeing the code out of context.
66	
67	For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
68	`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable the
69	project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.
70	
71	```rust
72	// Specific reason
73	let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
74	
75	// Self-evident
76	let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
77	
78	// Proven precondition
79	match matches.len() {
80	    1 => {
81	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
82	        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
83	        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
84	    }
85	    // ...
86	}
87	```
88	
89	Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the correct
90	test behavior.
91	
92	## Todo format
93	
94	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
95	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
96	references.
97	
98	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
99	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
100	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
101	
102	Examples:
103	
104	# Todo
105	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
106	- Fix bug Y [1]
107	
108	# Done
109	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
110	
111	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
112	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
113	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
114	
```

  "new_string": "/// Scan `jj bookmark list -a <name>` output for non-tracking remote refs.\n///\n/// Tracked remotes appear indented (`  @origin: ...`), non-tracked remotes\n/// appear at column 0 as `<bookmark>@<remote>: ...`. Returns the non-tracking\n/// remote name if any is found.\nfn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String> {\n    let prefix = format!(\"{bookmark}@\");\n    for line in list_output.lines() {\n        // Non-tracking remote ref is at column 0 and starts with \"<bookmark>@\"\n        if line.starts_with(&prefix)\n            && let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&prefix)\n            && let Some((remote, _)) = rest.split_once(':')\n        {\n            return Some(remote.to_string());\n        }\n    }\n    None\n}\n\n/// Validate inputs synchronously before detaching.\n///\n/// Catches the common failure modes up front so the parent can exit\n/// with a visible non-zero status — rather than discovering them in\n/// the detached child where errors only reach the log file.\nfn preflight(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"preflight: checking opts\");\n    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();\n    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(\".\");\n\n    // Verify squash revsets resolve to something.\n    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {\n        run(\n            \"jj\",\n            &[\n                \"log\", \"-r\", &sq.source, \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"\\\"\\\"\", \"-R\", &repo_str,\n            ],\n            cwd,\n        )\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"squash source '{}' does not resolve: {e}\", sq.source))?;\n        run(\n            \"jj\",\n            &[\n                \"log\", \"-r\", &sq.target, \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"\\\"\\\"\", \"-R\", &repo_str,\n            ],\n            cwd,\n        )\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"squash target '{}' does not resolve: {e}\", sq.target))?;\n    }\n\n    // Verify bookmark exists and (if a remote ref exists) is tracking it.\n    if let Some(ref bookmark) = opts.bookmark {\n        let exists = run(\"jj\", &[\"bookmark\", \"list\", bookmark, \"-R\", &repo_str], cwd)?;\n        if exists.is_empty() {\n            return Err(format!(\"bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist\").into());\n        }\n\n        let all = run(\n            \"jj\",\n            &[\"bookmark\", \"list\", \"-a\", bookmark, \"-R\", &repo_str],\n            cwd,\n        )?;\n        if let Some(remote) = find_non_tracking_remote(&all, bookmark) {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"bookmark '{bookmark}' has non-tracking remote '{bookmark}@{remote}' — \\\n                 run `jj bookmark track {bookmark}@{remote} -R {repo_str}` to fix\"\n            )\n            .into());\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn finalize_exec(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"finalize_exec: starting opts={opts:?}\");\n    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();\n    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(\".\");\n\n    // Squash if requested\n    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {"
}

1	# Chores-04
2	
3	## Audit `unwrap`/`unwrap_or` usage (0.32.0)
4	
5	Survey every `unwrap*` site in `src/` (non-test). Classify each, then
6	annotate with a trailing `// OK: …` comment that justifies why the
7	call is acceptable. This is a documentation pass — no behavioral
8	changes — and a convention we can extend to future code.
9	
10	### Comment convention
11	
12	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — when there's a real precondition,
13	  invariant, or domain reason worth capturing
14	- `// OK: obvious` — when the default is self-evident from context
15	  (e.g. `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title)
16	
17	Bare `// OK` is avoided because it reads like a truncated comment.
18	Abbreviations like `SE` are avoided because they require a decoder
19	ring for anyone reading the code out of context.
20	
21	Tests are left alone. `#[cfg(test)]` `.unwrap()` panics on failure,
22	which is the correct test behavior.
23	
24	### Documentation home
25	
26	Dev-facing conventions live in `notes/README.md` (alongside existing
27	"Versioning during development" and "Todo format" sections). User-facing
28	`/README.md` gets a small `## Contributing` section pointing at
29	`notes/`. `CLAUDE.md` adds a one-line reference so the bot sees the
30	same convention.
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "### Version\n\nSingle-step bump to `0.32.0`. Mechanical doc-only change, no behavior\ndifference, one commit.",
  "new_string": "### Version\n\nSingle-step bump to `0.32.0`. Mechanical doc-only change, no behavior\ndifference, one commit.\n\n## Make `finalize` failures visible (0.33.0)\n\n`finalize --detach --log <path>` hides failures: the parent spawns a\nchild and exits 0, the child's error only lands in the log file, and\nthe caller (interactive user or bot) never sees anything went wrong.\nReal incident: `jj git push` failed in the `.claude` repo with\n\"Non-tracking remote bookmark main@origin exists\" — parent returned 0,\nsession ended, nobody noticed until much later.\n\n### Requirements\n\n1. **Pre-flight validation in the parent**, before `detach()`:\n   squash revsets resolve, push bookmark exists, push bookmark is\n   tracking its remote ref. Synchronous non-zero exit with visible\n   stderr for this class of failures.\n2. **Subprocess output must be visible**: `common::run()` currently\n   demotes captured stdout/stderr to `debug!`, so without `-v` the\n   user never sees `jj`'s messages. Change to `info!` on success\n   output and `error!` on failure stderr.\n3. **Detached child output must reach the user's terminal when one\n   exists**: parent opens `/dev/tty` and passes it to the child as\n   stdout/stderr. Falls back to null when there is no controlling\n   terminal (pipe-invoked / bot / cron), relying on the log file +\n   status marker in that case.\n4. **Status marker file for post-detach failures**: child writes an\n   exit-code + last-error file on completion (alongside the log, or\n   in `~/.cache/vc-x1/`). Any subsequent `vc-x1` invocation reads\n   pending markers and surfaces failures to stderr, so the bot or user\n   sees the previous failure on their next command.\n\nEvery `CliLogger`-enabled record must reach *both* the terminal stream\nAND the log file (if `--log`). The existing routing already does this\nfor enabled levels — audit + document.\n\n### Plan (multi-step)\n\n- `0.33.0-dev1` — pre-flight validation in `finalize::preflight()`,\n  called before `detach()`. Covers bookmark existence + tracking,\n  squash revset resolution.\n- `0.33.0-dev2` — `common::run()` logging: `info!` for subprocess\n  output, `error!` for failure stderr, so failures surface without\n  `-v`.\n- `0.33.0-dev3` — `/dev/tty` reconnect in `detach()`: parent opens\n  the controlling terminal and passes it to the child, so a detached\n  child can still write to the user's shell. Null fallback when no\n  tty.\n- `0.33.0-dev4` — status marker file: child writes result on\n  completion, next `vc-x1` invocation consumes pending markers and\n  surfaces any failures prominently.\n- `0.33.0` — roll-up, notes finalize.\n\n### 0.33.0-dev1 — pre-flight validation\n\n`finalize::preflight()` runs before `detach()` and validates:\n\n- **Squash revsets resolve** (if `--squash`): `jj log -r <rev> --no-graph`\n  on both source and target. Errors surface as\n  `squash source '<rev>' does not resolve: …`.\n- **Bookmark exists** (if `--push`): `jj bookmark list <name>` must\n  return non-empty. Errors as `bookmark '<name>' does not exist`.\n- **Bookmark is tracking its remote** (if `--push`): scan\n  `jj bookmark list -a <name>` output. Tracked remotes appear indented\n  (`  @origin: …`); a non-tracked remote appears at column 0 as\n  `<bookmark>@<remote>: …`. If found, error with a remediation hint:\n  `run `jj bookmark track <bookmark>@<remote> -R <repo>` to fix`.\n\n`find_non_tracking_remote()` is a pure helper tested in unit tests\nagainst a few representative `jj bookmark list -a` output snippets.\n\nBookmark-existence is still re-checked inside `finalize_exec()` as\ndefense-in-depth — pre-flight catches it synchronously, the child\ncatches any unlikely race between pre-flight and execution."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/finalize.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn finalize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    // Nothing to do — show help hint\n    if opts.squash.is_none() && !opts.push {\n        return Err(\"nothing to do (see --help for options)\".into());\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"finalize: entry opts={opts:?}\");\n    let result = if opts.detach && !opts.exec {\n        detach(opts)\n    } else {\n        finalize_exec(opts)\n    };",
  "new_string": "pub fn finalize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    // Nothing to do — show help hint\n    if opts.squash.is_none() && !opts.push {\n        return Err(\"nothing to do (see --help for options)\".into());\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"finalize: entry opts={opts:?}\");\n\n    // Validate synchronously before detaching so failures exit with a\n    // visible non-zero status rather than hiding in the detached child's log.\n    // Skip in --exec (the re-entered child already passed preflight in the parent).\n    if !opts.exec {\n        preflight(opts)?;\n    }\n\n    let result = if opts.detach && !opts.exec {\n        detach(opts)\n    } else {\n        finalize_exec(opts)\n    };"
}

> AGENT

Two distinct asks. Let me think through each.

**1. Post-commit bookmark advance in the pre-commit/commit workflow**

The signal *was* in the commit output — `.claude`'s showed `main*` on `@-`, app repo's didn't. I missed it. Right fix: add a post-commit verification/advance step to `CLAUDE.md`'s commit workflow, applied per repo:

```
After each commit, verify the bookmark landed on @-:
  jj bookmark list <name> -R <repo>
If it didn't advance, set it:
  jj bookmark set <name> -r @- -R <repo>
```

For `.claude` this is usually a no-op (its config appears to auto-advance); for the app repo it's typically required. Making it an explicit post-commit step means the bot never forgets, and the user doesn't have to catch it again. I'll add this under a new "Post-commit" subsection in CLAUDE.md.

**2. Finalize should check repo state in pre-flight**

Agreed, and I'd layer it as two things:

**2a. Log planned actions before detaching** (at `info!`):
```
finalize: squash @ → @- in .claude
finalize: set bookmark 'main' from owwoulzo bf6fe3a7 to tnquuqku 12dae6b9
finalize: push 'main' to origin
```
Makes the dry-run obvious to the user before the child takes over. Catches surprises.

**2b. State sanity checks that error out if something's wrong:**
- **No conflicts in the working tree** — `jj st -R <repo>` parsed for conflict markers. Finalizing a conflicted repo is almost always a bug.
- **Bookmark move is forward-only** — the new bookmark target (squash target, usually `@-`) must be a descendant of the current bookmark position. If the bookmark is on a divergent branch (not an ancestor of `@-`), error out. Prevents accidental bookmark hops that would push unrelated history.
- **Squash target has a non-empty description** (for the push case) — the `.claude` session commit often hasn't been described yet mid-session; pushing it would fail anyway (`Won't push commit … since it has no description`). Catching this synchronously is more useful than seeing it 10s later in a log.

Want me to fold **2a + 2b** into dev1 (amend, since we just committed) or add a new **dev1.5**/extend scope for dev1? And should the CLAUDE.md post-commit workflow update land in dev1's same commit or its own?

51	## jj Basics
52	
53	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
54	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
55	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
56	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
57	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
58	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
59	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
60	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
61	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
62	  session because session data updates continuously.
63	
64	## Commit Message Style
65	
66	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
67	a version suffix:
68	
69	```
70	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
71	```
72	
73	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
74	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
```

116	/// Validate inputs synchronously before detaching.
117	///
118	/// Catches the common failure modes up front so the parent can exit
119	/// with a visible non-zero status — rather than discovering them in
120	/// the detached child where errors only reach the log file.
121	fn preflight(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
122	    debug!("preflight: checking opts");
123	    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();
124	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
125	
126	    // Verify squash revsets resolve to something.
127	    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {
128	        run(
129	            "jj",
130	            &[
131	                "log",
132	                "-r",
133	                &sq.source,
134	                "--no-graph",
135	                "-T",
136	                "\"\"",
137	                "-R",
138	                &repo_str,
139	            ],
140	            cwd,
141	        )
142	        .map_err(|e| format!("squash source '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.source))?;
143	        run(
144	            "jj",
145	            &[
146	                "log",
147	                "-r",
148	                &sq.target,
149	                "--no-graph",
150	                "-T",
151	                "\"\"",
152	                "-R",
153	                &repo_str,
154	            ],
155	            cwd,
156	        )
157	        .map_err(|e| format!("squash target '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.target))?;
158	    }
159	
160	    // Verify bookmark exists and (if a remote ref exists) is tracking it.
161	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = opts.bookmark {
162	        let exists = run("jj", &["bookmark", "list", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str], cwd)?;
163	        if exists.is_empty() {
164	            return Err(format!("bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist").into());
165	        }
166	
167	        let all = run(
168	            "jj",
169	            &["bookmark", "list", "-a", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
170	            cwd,
171	        )?;
172	        if let Some(remote) = find_non_tracking_remote(&all, bookmark) {
173	            return Err(format!(
174	                "bookmark '{bookmark}' has non-tracking remote '{bookmark}@{remote}' — \
175	                 run `jj bookmark track {bookmark}@{remote} -R {repo_str}` to fix"
176	            )
177	            .into());
178	        }
179	    }
180	
181	    Ok(())
182	}

],\n            cwd,\n        )?;\n        if range.is_empty() {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"bookmark '{bookmark}' move is not forward — current position is not an \\\n                 ancestor of '{target_rev}' (would diverge)\"\n            )\n            .into());\n        }\n\n        if opts.push {\n            let desc = run(\n                \"jj\",\n                &[\n                    \"log\",\n                    \"-r\",\n                    target_rev,\n                    \"--no-graph\",\n                    \"-T\",\n                    \"description\",\n                    \"-R\",\n                    &repo_str,\n                ],\n                cwd,\n            )?;\n            if desc.trim().is_empty() {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"push target '{target_rev}' has no description — push would fail \\\n                     (run `jj describe -r {target_rev} -R {repo_str}` first)\"\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    log_plan(opts)?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Return Ok(()) if the revset resolves to one or more commits in `repo`.\nfn jj_rev_exists(repo: &str, cwd: &std::path::Path, rev: &str) -> Result<(), String> {\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"log\", \"-r\", rev, \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"\\\"x\\\"\", \"-R\", repo],\n        cwd,\n    )\n    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Log what finalize is about to do so the user sees the plan before detach.\nfn log_plan(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();\n    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(\".\");\n\n    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {\n        info!(\n            \"finalize: squash {} → {} in {}\",\n            sq.source, sq.target, repo_str\n        );\n    }\n    if let Some(ref bookmark) = opts.bookmark {\n        let target_rev = opts\n            .squash\n            .as_ref()\n            .map(|sq| sq.target.as_str())\n            .unwrap_or(\"@\"); // OK: same as above\n        let current = jj_rev_short(&repo_str, cwd, bookmark).unwrap_or_else(|_| \"?\".into()); // OK: logging only — fall back to \"?\" if revset fails\n        let target = jj_rev_short(&repo_str, cwd, target_rev).unwrap_or_else(|_| \"?\".into()); // OK: same\n        info!(\n            \"finalize: set bookmark '{bookmark}' {current} → {target} ({target_rev})\"\n        );\n        if opts.push {\n            info!(\"finalize: push '{bookmark}' to remote\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Short one-line summary of a revset: `<change_short> <commit_short>`.\nfn jj_rev_short(repo: &str, cwd: &std::path::Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, String> {\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\",\n            \"-r\",\n            rev,\n            \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\",\n            \"change_id.shortest(8) ++ \\\" \\\" ++ commit_id.shortest(8)\",\n            \"-R\",\n            repo,\n        ],\n        cwd,\n    )\n    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "Bookmark-existence is still re-checked inside `finalize_exec()` as\ndefense-in-depth — pre-flight catches it synchronously, the child\ncatches any unlikely race between pre-flight and execution.",
  "new_string": "Bookmark-existence is still re-checked inside `finalize_exec()` as\ndefense-in-depth — pre-flight catches it synchronously, the child\ncatches any unlikely race between pre-flight and execution.\n\n### 0.33.0-dev1.1 — repo-state checks + plan logging + post-commit workflow\n\nTwo findings after dev1 landed:\n\n1. After `jj commit -R .`, the app repo's `main` bookmark did not\n   advance to `@-` (the `.claude` repo's config does advance it, the\n   app repo's does not). Easy to miss — the `Parent commit (@-)`\n   line in `jj commit` output shows `<bookmark>* |` when the bookmark\n   sits on that commit; when absent, the bookmark is stuck behind.\n2. `finalize` pre-flight should also sanity-check the target repo\n   state before detaching, and should *log the plan* so the user\n   knows what's about to happen.\n\n**CLAUDE.md addition** — new \"Post-commit: advance the bookmark\"\nsubsection under the commit-style section. For every `jj commit`:\n\n- `jj bookmark list <name> -R <repo>` — verify position\n- `jj bookmark set <name> -r @- -R <repo>` — advance if stuck\n\n**Finalize pre-flight additions** (`finalize::preflight()`):\n\n- **No conflicts** — `jj log -r 'conflicts()' …` must be empty.\n  Refuses to finalize a repo with unresolved conflicts.\n- **Forward-only bookmark move** — `jj log -r '<bookmark>::(<target>)'`\n  must be non-empty. If the current bookmark position is not an\n  ancestor of the post-finalize target, we'd diverge; error out.\n  `(…)` around the target protects `@-` and similar from being\n  parsed as a suffix of the bookmark name.\n- **Push target has a description** (if `--push`) — `jj log -r <target>\n  -T description` must be non-empty. Otherwise `jj git push` would\n  fail with `Won't push commit … since it has no description`.\n\n**Plan logging** (`finalize::log_plan()`, called at end of preflight):\n\n```\nfinalize: squash @ → @- in <repo>\nfinalize: set bookmark 'main' <current-change> <current-commit> → <target-change> <target-commit> (<target-rev>)\nfinalize: push 'main' to remote\n```\n\n`info!` level so it's always visible. Uses jj templating\n(`change_id.shortest(8) ++ \" \" ++ commit_id.shortest(8)`) for short\nIDs. Minor helpers `jj_rev_exists()` and `jj_rev_short()` keep the\npreflight body readable."
}
```

One caveat worth flagging: my retest for the "happy path" case (`vc-x1 finalize --squash --push main --repo .claude` with no `--detach`) wasn't a dry-run — it actually squashed `.claude`'s working copy into `@-` and pushed main. That's why `.claude` advanced through dev1 mid-session. Safe in outcome (it's the same thing finalize would have done at session end), but a live side-effect rather than a read-only check. I should have used `--detach` or a throwaway repo for the retest.

104	fn main() -> ExitCode {

107

110

113	        Commands::Desc(desc_args) => run_command(desc::desc(&desc_args)),
114	        Commands::List(list_args) => run_command(list::list(&list_args)),
115	        Commands::Show(show_args) => run_command(show::show(&show_args)),

122	        Commands::Symlink(symlink_args) => run_command(symlink::symlink(&symlink_args)),

126	                Err(e) => {
127	                    error!("{e}");
128	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
129	                }
130	            };
131	            run_command(finalize::finalize(&opts))
132	        }
133	    }

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Run a shell command. Logs the command and output at debug level.\n/// Returns stdout on success.\npub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let args_str = args.join(\" \");\n    debug!(\"$ {cmd} {args_str}\");\n    trace!(\"cwd: {}\", cwd.display());\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(cwd)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run {cmd}: {e}\"))?;\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();\n    if !stdout.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"  {stdout}\");\n    }\n    if !stderr.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"  {stderr}\");\n    }\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    Ok(stdout)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.\n///\n/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:\n/// - **stderr at `info!`** on success — jj prints human-readable messages\n///   (`Moved 1 bookmarks to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) to\n///   stderr, and the user needs to see them without requiring `-v`.\n/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data\n///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), so `info!` would flood the user\n///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.\n/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's\n///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.\npub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let args_str = args.join(\" \");\n    debug!(\"$ {cmd} {args_str}\");\n    trace!(\"cwd: {}\", cwd.display());\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(cwd)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run {cmd}: {e}\"))?;\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();\n    if !stdout.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"  {stdout}\");\n    }\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    if !stderr.is_empty() {\n        info!(\"{stderr}\");\n    }\n    Ok(stdout)\n}"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "**Plan logging** (`finalize::log_plan()`, called at end of preflight):\n\n```\nfinalize: squash @ → @- in <repo>\nfinalize: set bookmark 'main' <current-change> <current-commit> → <target-change> <target-commit> (<target-rev>)\nfinalize: push 'main' to remote\n```\n\n`info!` level so it's always visible. Uses jj templating\n(`change_id.shortest(8) ++ \" \" ++ commit_id.shortest(8)`) for short\nIDs. Minor helpers `jj_rev_exists()` and `jj_rev_short()` keep the\npreflight body readable.",
  "new_string": "**Plan logging** (`finalize::log_plan()`, called at end of preflight):\n\n```\nfinalize: squash @ → @- in <repo>\nfinalize: set bookmark 'main' <current-change> <current-commit> → <target-change> <target-commit> (<target-rev>)\nfinalize: push 'main' to remote\n```\n\n`info!` level so it's always visible. Uses jj templating\n(`change_id.shortest(8) ++ \" \" ++ commit_id.shortest(8)`) for short\nIDs. Minor helpers `jj_rev_exists()` and `jj_rev_short()` keep the\npreflight body readable.\n\n### 0.33.0-dev2 — subprocess output visibility in `common::run()`\n\n`common::run()` previously demoted both captured stdout and stderr to\n`debug!`, so without `-v` the user saw nothing from invoked subprocesses\non success. jj's human-readable messages (`Moved 1 bookmarks to …`,\n`Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`, push summaries) go to **stderr**,\nwhile data output (bookmark lists, commit IDs) goes to **stdout**. The\nfix follows that split:\n\n- **stderr at `info!` on success** — the user sees what jj actually did.\n- **stdout at `debug!`** — callers consume it as data; `info!` would\n  flood the user with output they didn't ask for (bookmark lists,\n  revset queries).\n- **Failure path unchanged** — `run()` returns `Err` with stderr in the\n  message; `main::run_command` already logs it at `error!`.\n\nNo new tests: the behavior is in the shape of a log call (routing via\nthe `log` crate), the logger already has unit tests, and the subprocess\nside is exercised end-to-end during `vc-x1 finalize` retests. Adding a\nunit test here would require mocking `std::process::Command`, which is\nmore noise than signal."
}
```

"feat: surface subprocess stderr without -v (0.33.0-dev2)" \
-m "common::run() previously demoted captured stdout and stderr to
debug!, so without -v the user saw nothing from invoked
subprocesses on success. Split by stream convention:

- stderr → info! on success — jj's human-readable messages
  ('Moved 1 bookmarks to …', 'Nothing changed.', push summaries)
  now surface by default.
- stdout → debug! — callers consume it as data; info! would
  flood the user with bookmark lists and revset query results.

Failure path unchanged — Err propagates stderr in its message;
main::run_command logs it at error!.

ochid: /.claude/okynxqnvtsky" \
-R .
```

Approve?

> DEVELOPER

I don't see any changes associated with logs showing up on the termainl and nothing associated with attach, maybe I just don't know where to look

> AGENT

You're right — dev2 scope is narrow, and the "detach/tty reconnect" work is dev3 (not yet done). Let me point at the concrete dev2 change and show the before/after:

**One-line diff in `src/common.rs` `run()`:**

```
-    if !stderr.is_empty() {
-        debug!("  {stderr}");
-    }
     if !output.status.success() {
         return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
     }
+    if !stderr.is_empty() {
+        info!("{stderr}");
+    }
```

**Where the effect showed up** — in the retest output earlier:
```
finalize: squash @ → @- in …         ← log_plan (dev1.1, info!)
finalize: set bookmark 'main' …      ← log_plan
finalize: push 'main' to remote      ← log_plan
Working copy (@) now at: …           ← jj squash stderr, now info! (was debug!)   ← dev2
Nothing changed.                      ← jj bookmark set stderr, now info!          ← dev2
Changes to push to origin: …         ← jj git push stderr, now info!              ← dev2
  Move forward bookmark main …
```

Before dev2 those last four lines were silent without `-v`; now they're always visible.

**Nothing detach-related in dev2** — that's intentional. The full plan:
- dev1 ✅ pre-flight
- dev1.1 ✅ repo-state + plan logging + CLAUDE.md post-commit
- **dev2 (this one) ✅ `common::run()` stderr → info!**
- **dev3 (next) — `/dev/tty` reconnect for detached child** ← the attach/detach change
- dev4 — status marker file

Want to approve dev2 as a scoped commit, then move to dev3? Or fold more into dev2?

> DEVELOPER

ok this is fine, but one more thing it looks like you're using .claude for testing, not a good thing, we need to make appropriate test repos either permanately in a ./test-data/ or in /tmp or ..., thoughts

> AGENT

Good catch — I shouldn't be running finalize against live `.claude` or `.` for retests. Options:

**A. Shell script** `scripts/make-test-repo.sh` — creates `/tmp/vc-x1-test-XXX/` with jj repo + bare-git remote on demand. Quick to write, no Rust changes. I pass `--repo /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXX/work` to finalize during retests.

**B. Rust subcommand** `vc-x1 test-fixture [--path PATH]` — same setup, but as a subcommand. More discoverable, easier to evolve alongside init/clone, but more code.

**C. Gitignored `./test-repos/`** — persistent, inspectable between sessions, but risks divergence across machines and rots if not re-created cleanly.

**D. Integration tests via `tempfile` in `tests/`** — already on the todo list (`Add integration tests in tests/ for subcommands using temp jj repos`). Covers automated testing but doesn't help with interactive retest sessions.

My recommendation: **A + D as separate concerns.** A is the immediate pragmatic fix for manual retests during dev3/dev4 (just a small script). D stays on the todo list as the long-term automated-test plan.

**Where to put it version-wise?**
- Fold into **0.33.0-dev2.1** (it's infrastructure for the dev3/dev4 retests we're about to do), OR
- Separate **0.33.1** / its own chore, since it's test hygiene not finalize-visibility.

I'd go with **0.33.0-dev2.1** — it enables clean testing of dev3/dev4 without live side effects.

Thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

I say -dev2.1 using B discoverable and I like rust code better than scripts

39	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
40	fn run_retry(
41	    cmd: &str,
42	    args: &[&str],
43	    cwd: &Path,
44	    retries: u32,
45	    delay_secs: u64,
46	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
47	    let mut last_err = String::new();
48	    for attempt in 1..=retries {
49	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
50	            Ok(out) => {
51	                if attempt > 1 {
52	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
53	                }
54	                return Ok(out);
55	            }
56	            Err(e) => {
57	                last_err = e.to_string();
58	                if attempt < retries {
59	                    debug!("attempt {attempt}/{retries} failed: {last_err}");
60	                    debug!("retrying in {delay_secs}s...");
61	                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));
62	                }
63	            }
64	        }
65	    }
66	    Err(format!("failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}").into())
67	}

71	/// Get the short (12-char) jj change ID for a revision, without printing.
72	fn jj_chid(rev: &str, cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
73	    let full = run(
74	        "jj",
75	        &[
76	            "log",
77	            "-r",
78	            rev,
79	            "--no-graph",
80	            "-T",
81	            "change_id",
82	            "--limit",
83	            "1",
84	        ],
85	        cwd,
86	    )?;
87	    Ok(full[..full.len().min(12)].to_string())
88	}
